# Proposed OEIS comments: A251113 to A252672

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A251113

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251113>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2475](../papers/2001-2500/2475-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence for column k = 1 is true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A251114

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 26. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251158

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251158>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [351](../papers/0001-0500/0351-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251257

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251257>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [284](../papers/0001-0500/0284-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 49 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 49, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. ```
```

## A251397

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251397>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [179](../papers/0001-0500/0179-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 81 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 81, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251612>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [282](../papers/0001-0500/0282-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 49 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 343 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 49, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251645

Paper: [7675](../papers/7501-8000/7675-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 2*a(n-2) + 5*a(n-3) - 7*a(n-4) - 12*a(n-5) + 6*a(n-6) + 6*a(n-7) for n>14.
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(188 + 36*x - 231*x^2 - 1133*x^3 + 25*x^4 + 1610*x^5 + 60*x^6 - 494*x^7 - 40*x^8 + 12*x^9 + 40*x^10 + 6*x^11 - 18*x^12 - 6*x^13) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - x - x^2)*(1 - 6*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 30 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 161 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 14. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
